# restedXP_questing versioning (was Master Farmer - Grindbot)

Copy-forward. Never rename or delete an old version folder.

## Identity

Canonical fields live in `version.lua`. `header.lua`, the GUI title, and the load log must match that table.

## On a material change

1. Bump `version.lua` and every `-- Version:` banner.
2. `python make_local.py` - syntax-checks every .lua, regenerates `manifest.lua`, and copies the plugin to `_MASTER-FARMER_/Grindbot_Versions/restedXP_questing_vX.Y.Z/`. An existing version folder is never overwritten without `--force`.
3. Test in game through the Master Farmer launcher (the only loader).
4. Commit as `vX.Y.Z: <summary>` and push `sentinel-nav`. **Do NOT open a PR to `main` and do NOT merge.**
   (audit F8, 2026-10-28: this step used to say to push `dev` and merge to `main` because that is what
   the game loads. It is not. `plugin/Master_Farmer/mfcore/catalog.lua` loads RestedXP Questing from
   `branch = "sentinel-nav"`, so pushing this branch IS publishing, and `main` is the archived
   pre-Sentinel MasterArthasNav build - merging into it would overwrite the thing it exists to keep.
   See `products_src/restedXP_questing/FROZEN.md`.)
5. Do not bump for comment-only or rule-only edits.

**One loader only.** The Master Farmer launcher loads this bot from GitHub `sentinel-nav`
(audit F8, 2026-10-28: it never loaded `main`). The standalone `plugin_loader/` and `plugin_loader.zip` were retired in 3.4.1 (a copy is in `_MASTER-FARMER_\projects-old\grindbot_plugin_loader`); never add a loader folder back.

## Revert

Disable the newer plugin in Sylvanas and enable the older `restedXP_questing_vX.Y.Z` folder.

## History

| Version | Change |
| --- | --- |
| 1.3.38 | movement.lua split into movement/*; HTTP plugin_loader added. Preserved as tag `working-v1.3.38`. |
| 1.4.1 | Priest rotation added and registered. death.lua: killer blacklist, level-gap threat filter, ground-snapped safe-spot candidates. movement.ground_z exported. rotation.lua registry generalised. |
| 1.4.2 | Druid and Paladin rotations added. Paladin aura is one dropdown, not six toggles. Fixed: class heal-threshold sliders were in the checkbox alias table, so `gui.slider` always returned the fallback. Added `gui.combo` and a `combo_aliases` table. |
| 1.4.3 | Auto-equip added (`equip.lua`). Vendor purchasing NOT implemented: the known `core.input`/`core.game_ui` surface exposes no buy function. |
| 1.4.4 | Vendor food/drink buying (`supplies.lua`) - the API reference confirmed `core.input.buy_item` and `core.game_ui.get_vendor_item_info` exist, correcting the v1.4.3 claim. Fixed: auto-equip never confirmed the bind-on-equip prompt, so BoE upgrades were re-issued forever and never worn. |
| 1.4.5 | Hunter, Warlock, Shaman and Rogue rotations plus shared `pets.lua`. All nine classes now supported. Resolves NEXT_FEATURES 5.3 (weapon enchants) and 5.4 (pets). Rogue poison *application* still has no API. |
| 1.4.6 | Eat/drink: one consumable at a time. Added a 5s commit window (aura latency was double-consuming), split the shared food/drink timer, capped uses per rest and warn when an aura id is missing instead of silently eating the stack. |
| 1.4.7 | Rest now outranks looting in the tick cascade - loot.tick returning true every tick while a corpse was in range meant healing.tick was never reached and the bot never ate or drank. Added resting guards to loot.tick, rotation.tick and rotation.buffs_ooc. |
| 1.4.8 | Fixed: the v1.4.6 per-rest use cap (`MAX_USES = 8`) was only ever cleared by `clear_rest()`, which runs on player-nil, toggle-off and swimming - never on a rest that simply finished. The counter therefore accumulated across the whole session and eating and drinking stopped permanently after eight consumables. The budget is now cleared wherever a rest latch drops. |
| 1.4.9 | Eat/drink could stall with no output at all. `use_self_safe` applies nine gates by default and one of them is the global cooldown, which food and drink do not use - a rest entered straight after a kill was refused for the whole GCD. `use_first` returned a bare `false`, `consume_one` swallowed it, and `healing.tick` still reported "resting", so the bot sat doing nothing and logged nothing. Now: `skip_gcd` is passed, a refused use falls back to the unguarded `use_self`, every failure is named in a warning once per rest, and `mfg_rest_debug` prints the single gate currently blocking a rest. Also hardened the module-load item prewarm, which called `izi.item` unguarded ~250 times - one throw would have failed the chunk and taken the whole bot down with it. |
| 1.5.0 | Quest dialog handling rewritten against the real `core.quests` surface. Three defects: (1) `complete_quest()` and `get_quest_reward(0)` were called in sequence, but they are alternatives - `get_quest_reward(i)` selects choice i *and* completes - so a quest offering a choice of rewards could never be handed in; the reward is now chosen by vendor value and `complete_quest()` is used only when there is no choice. (2) `confirm_accept_quest()` was never called, so escort / auto-accept quests stalled on their confirmation popup. (3) Only the gossip frame was handled; an NPC showing the quest *greeting* frame (indexed, via `get_available_title` / `select_available_quest`) fell through to a bare `accept_quest()` with nothing selected. `decline_quest`, `abandon_quest`, `add_quest_watch` and the trainer calls exist but have no caller in this bot and were deliberately left unused. |
| 1.5.1 | Quest rewards were never chosen. `go_and_interact` re-issued `interact_with_object` every 1.2s while the bot stood at the NPC, and the dialog steps only ran on the tick that fired - but re-interacting tears down the open frame, so the reward frame never survived long enough for `get_quest_item_link` to report a choice. Every hand-in fell through to `complete_quest()`, which the client refuses while a choice is pending, and the quest stayed in the log for ever. Walking to the NPC (`npc.at_npc`) and driving the dialog (`npc.accept` / `npc.turn_in`) are now separate; each dialog is a tick-driven state machine that interacts ONCE, waits for the frame, reads the choices, picks one and verifies the quest left the log, retrying up to three times before warning. Reward choice is now rated by `equip.rate`: a usable upgrade beats a usable item, beats a non-equippable one, beats something the class cannot use, with vendor price only breaking ties. Added the class weapon-proficiency and numeric armour tables that make "usable" mean something - a Mage no longer takes the plate chest because it sells for more. New `mfg_quest_debug` logs every step and every choice considered. |
| 1.5.2 | The gossip path never worked on TBC. On Classic Era and TBC Classic the legacy client sends no quest id with a gossip list, so `get_gossip_*_quests` fills `quest_id` with the **1-based row index**. The code compared that against a real quest id (never matched) and then passed a real quest id to `select_gossip_*_quest` (addressed a row that does not exist), so nothing was ever selected - the bot only worked when the quest detail frame happened to already be up. Gossip rows are now matched on **title**, with the id comparison kept as a retail fallback, and the opaque handle from the same frame is passed straight back to the selector. `npc.is_complete` had the same bug in its gossip fallback. Also: `entry.is_complete` is declared an integer by the API reference and a boolean by the documentation, so both are accepted, and `-1` (failed) is no longer treated as in-progress. `vendor.lua` was already correct - it matches on `gossip_type` and round-trips `gossip_option_id`. |
| 1.6.0 | Two features. **Skip grey quests**: `npc.accept` checks the gossip row's `is_trivial` before selecting and, when set, puts the quest in the same skip bag the GUI's manual skip uses so `pick` moves on. A greeting-frame NPC has no `is_trivial`, so it falls back to `get_available_level` with a conservative five-level gap. Skipped rather than declined - `decline_quest` only dismisses the current frame, so the bot would be offered the quest again on the next pass. Checkbox "Skip Grey Quests" on the quest tab, on by default. **Trainer** (`trainer.lua`): when a gossip frame is open at an NPC that trains this class, buy every affordable spell rank cheapest-first. Class spells only - anything with a `talent_cost` or `profession_cost` is left alone. It does NOT travel: there is no trainer location data in this project and inventing coordinates would send the bot walking into scenery, so it trains opportunistically at trainers the bot is already talking to. Checkbox "Train Spells" on the settings tab, OFF by default, plus a gold-reserve slider so training cannot empty the purse. Sits ahead of `vendor.tick` in the cascade because both want the gossip frame. Also fixed a `npc.is_complete` caller in `quest.snapshot` that was still missing the quest name after 1.5.2. |
| 1.6.1 | **Alliance 1-60 w/Vendoring**: all 27 PathTool JSON routes from `Alliance 1-60 vendoring/1-60_LVLGRIND` converted to Lua under `grind/paths/ally160/`, 9,223 waypoints, verified bit-for-bit against the source JSON. New menu list "Alliance 1-60 w/Vendoring" (region key `ally160`), lazily loaded through the existing `path_format.take_module`, which holds one route at a time and drops the previous one - the index is 10.6 KB and the routes it points at are 0.5 MB, so none are loaded until selected. Loop flag derived from geometry (first/last waypoint within 30 yards), since every source file declared `loop:false` regardless. Vendor NPC ids read from each folder's `README.txt` and attached as `merchant = { npc_id, ids }`; 15 of 27 routes carry one. Looping vendor routes also carry `vendor_each_lap`, and a completed lap now triggers a sell/repair stop on top of the usual full-bags and broken-gear triggers - those routes start and end at their vendor, so the stop costs no extra travel. `path_runner` gained a lap counter with a one-shot `take_lap()`; `vendor.lua` resolves the running path's merchant ahead of the zone default and will accept any of the listed npc ids. Checkbox "Vendor Every Lap" on the vendor tab, on by default. Merchant coordinates are the route's first waypoint, not surveyed vendor positions - the npc id identifies the merchant, the coordinates only give the bot somewhere to return to. |
| 1.6.2 | The Alliance 1-60 routes did not appear in the menu. `grind/catalog` was not in `main.lua`'s `PLUGIN_MODULES` reload list, so a hot reload reused the previous session's index table and the new route list never showed up however many times the plugin was reloaded - the failure was completely invisible. All five path indexes (`grind/catalog`, `grind/paths/catalog`, `grind/paths/ally160/catalog`, `path_catalog`, `data/paths/catalog`) are now cleared on reload; they are a few kilobytes each. `add_index` also swallowed a failed require, producing an empty menu with no explanation, and now logs a warning instead. Fixed a stale region map in `gui.sync_profile_list` that still said `custom == 4` after a fifth region was inserted, so restoring a saved custom path selected the wrong list - it is derived from `REGION_KEYS` now. |
| 1.6.3 | API audit of the whole codebase against the `.api` reference and the reflected runtime dump: 98 distinct `core.*` calls, 16 `izi.*` calls, 94 method names, 689 declared signatures, table-return handling, argument counts and load-time safety. `core.*` was clean. Three fixes. **`core.input.pet_attack()` was called with no argument** but the API declares `pet_attack(target)` - inside a `pcall`, so the pet was never commanded onto the target and nothing said so; Hunter and Warlock pets only fought what hit them first. **`unit:health_current()` / `unit:health_max()`** in druid, paladin and priest do not exist; the fallback was dead code that would have reported permanent full health had `health_pct` ever failed, and now uses `get_health_percentage` then `get_health`/`get_max_health`. **`player:get_combo_points(target)`** exists on neither source, so the rogue's first combo-point read always threw and was swallowed; `combo_points_current()` is the documented name and is now tried first, with `get_combo_points_target()` kept as the fallback. |
| 1.7.0 | Memory. Measured, not guessed: **2,281 KB resident before, 1,559 KB after - 722 KB / 32% less** with the plugin installed and one grind route loaded. Three changes. (1) `path_format.normalize` wrote `wait = 0` and `combo = false` onto every waypoint. A waypoint is a hash table and Lua rounds the hash part to a power of two, so three keys cost four slots and five keys cost eight - writing defaults nobody reads **doubled** every loaded route. Every reader already guarded with `type(wp.wait) == "number"` or `wp.combo == true`, so the defaults are left nil: a loaded route is 36% smaller (802-waypoint Elwynn herb route 224 KB -> 143 KB). (2) `net_loader` compiled all 105 files during install and kept every chunk in `package.preload` for the session - a cost lazy loading never touched, because dropping a route from `package.loaded` frees the waypoint table but not the chunk that builds it. It now keeps the source and compiles on demand, letting the chunk go afterwards: 2,056 KB -> 1,416 KB. Every file is still compile-checked at install, so a syntax error still aborts the swap-over. (3) `leveling_paths.lua` and `data/_zones_raw.lua` are unreachable from `main.lua` (verified by transitive trace) and `_zones_raw` was the only thing pulling all four race zone tables at once, so both are excluded from the manifest. Also fixed: the v1.6.1 converter silently dropped `wait`, `combo` and `actions` from the generated routes - one waypoint in the Duskwood 20-24 route lost a 2s wait and a spell action. The converter now preserves them, and only omits them when they are at their defaults. |
| 1.8.0 | Six GUI and rotation changes. **Removed**: the mage Ice Barrier and Wand checkboxes plus the logic behind them, and Teleport Detect entirely (checkbox, distance slider, `teleport_halt` in main and `state.teleport`). **Racials**: new `data/racials.lua` and `racials.lua` add nine active racials, each with a Class-tab checkbox filtered by a new `race_id` option mirroring the existing `class_id` filter in `ui.lua`. Offensive racials fire once a fight is under way rather than on the pull, mana and heal racials on their thresholds, and the escapes on the real `is_rooted` / `is_feared` state. Passives, Shadowmeld, Perception, Find Treasure and Cannibalize are deliberately excluded. **Resting moved to the rotations**: new `resting.lua` holds the shared machinery, each rotation gets `rest(player)` supplying its own thresholds, defaulting to **30%** health and mana. `rotation.rest` dispatches; `healing.lua` is reduced to combat potions plus the gate, and its Eat/Drink checkbox on the Healing tab is what lets the loaded rotation's resting run at all. `healing.is_resting()` is kept and forwards, so loot, rotation, the grind and quest engines and main are unchanged. `resting.lua` requires `rotation` lazily to avoid the cycle rotation -> class -> resting -> rotation. **Per-class scan range**: every rotation gets `scan_range(player)` - melee 20 yards, casters and hunters 35, with Druid and Shaman following their own melee toggle. `main.lua` uses it, a path's `pull` value still overrides, and the now-redundant Combat Scan slider is gone. |
| 1.8.1 | Eastern Kingdoms now lists all 27 Alliance 1-60 w/Vendoring routes as well as its own, deduplicated by id. Every one of those routes is in an Eastern Kingdoms zone, so they belong under that heading; the named list stays, because it is the one place they appear grouped and in level order. Deduplication is load-bearing rather than defensive: the original eight Eastern Kingdoms routes were converted from the same PathTool JSON as eight of the ally160 ones and share their ids, so without it each would appear twice under nearly identical labels. The ally160 copy wins, because it is the one that knows about the route's vendor. Implemented in `grind/catalog.lua` as an `INCLUDES` table so another region can borrow a list the same way later. Note: `grind/paths/` had been overwritten with copies of the ally160 files, which left every entry claiming `region = "ally160"` - Eastern Kingdoms was empty and the named list showed all 27 twice. That folder was restored from the previous commit and the 27 byte-identical copies removed; the routes themselves are untouched in `grind/paths/ally160/`. |
| 1.9.0 | **Grinding and Questing are now main-window tabs.** The profile chooser was only ever reachable through a launcher popup, which is why profiles appeared to be missing: the main window had General / Class / Mode / Healing / Settings and nothing else. Both new tabs open with their enable checkbox - a registered control, so the tab system draws it above the custom page - and the page below is inert until it is ticked, saying so rather than showing dead controls. **Continent is gone, replaced by Faction.** Continent was the wrong axis: every route that exists is Eastern Kingdoms, so three of the four choices were always empty while the question a player actually has - which side am I levelling - was never asked. All 35 catalog entries are tagged `faction = "alliance"`, `grind/catalog` gains `entries_for_faction` / `labels_for_faction` / `load_faction` / `faction_counts`, and a loaded path carries its faction so the selection restores correctly. New `data/factions.lua` maps race to side and the selector defaults to the character's own faction on the first tick, so an Alliance character opens on Alliance routes without touching anything. Horde shows a count of zero and says plainly that no Horde profiles exist yet and how to add one, rather than an empty dropdown. The Grinding page shows the chosen route's level range, waypoint count, whether it loops, and whether it has a vendor on it. |
| 1.9.1 | **Alliance routes were not showing because 1.9.0 auto-selected the character's own faction.** On a Horde character that landed on the Horde list, which has nothing in it, so the profiles looked as though they had vanished. `gui.sync_faction` now refuses to select a side with no routes, falls back to one that has some, and logs which detection answered and why it moved. The Grinding page also shows both sides' counts, so an empty list reads as "this side has none" rather than "the profiles are gone". **Faction detection uses both APIs as requested**: `unit:get_race_id()` first because a race maps to exactly one side and never changes, then `unit:get_faction_id()` as corroboration and as the fallback for the ticks before the player object can answer. Player faction template ids for all ten TBC races are mapped. Note: both are `game_object` methods - `unit_helper` carries no faction or race call at all, and `targeting.lua` already uses it for the enemy sweep it does provide. **Fixed a 1.9.0 regression**: `entries_for_faction` deduplicated in raw catalog order, so the eight legacy Eastern Kingdoms entries shadowed their ally160 twins - losing the vendor data and the level ordering. It now prefers the ally160 copy, matching `entries_for_region`. 15 of 27 routes carry vendor ids again. **Engine verified, not changed**: `path_to_zone` already carries coords, mobs, pull, merchant and map_id, and `targeting.id_wanted` treats a nil or empty mob list as "any npc" - which is correct for an area loop, since the level band, tap rules and reachability checks still apply. Documented in place so it is not "fixed" later. |
| 1.9.2 | Game Events audit. The project never called `core.register_on_game_event_callback` at all, and of the four confirmations the client **holds open until answered**, only the equip pair was being handled - by polling the latched `core.game_ui.get_pending_equip_slot()`, which the documentation explicitly blesses. **`LOOT_BIND_CONFIRM` was unanswered, and there is no latched reader for it**: the event is the only place the loot slot number exists. So every bind-on-pickup drop was left on the corpse while `loot.lua` burned its three attempts and gave up - on a levelling grind bot, precisely the loot worth having. New `events.lua` registers one callback using the documented handler-table dispatch and answers `LOOT_BIND_CONFIRM` with the **1-based to 0-based conversion** the docs warn about twice (forwarding `args[1]` straight through confirms the slot *after* the one that asked), plus both equip confirms on the edge and `CONFIRM_BINDER` so it can never hang. Registration is guarded on `_G.MasterFarmer_Grindbot`, not a module local: the callback cap is per plugin and this plugin hot-reloads by clearing `package.loaded`, so a module-local guard would re-register on every reload until the cap raised a Lua error. The handler table is re-read through the namespace, so a reload still runs new handler code through the original callback. The cast, aura and combat-log families are deliberately **not** registered - the documentation is explicit that a rotation should poll `game_object` for what a unit is casting, which every rotation here already does. |
| 1.9.3 | **Most of the route list was unreachable, and the cause was in the rendering layer every previous test had stubbed out.** `gui.path_index()` read the selection back out of the `mfg_path` COMBOBOX ELEMENT. That element is created with a single placeholder item and its item list is only refreshed by `sync_profile_list`, which the new Grinding tab never calls - so a real combobox widget, which cannot select an index it has no item for, clamped every `set()` to whatever stale count it was holding. The dropdown listed all 27 routes and picking anything past the clamp snapped straight back to the first few. Reproduced with a harness that drives the real `ui.lua` with a widget that clamps like the client's: selecting route 14 returned 8. The selection is now a module local, clamped against the live label count, with the element mirrored (items first, then value) only for the older popups - nothing reads the selection back out of it. All 27 indices round-trip, out-of-range clamps cleanly, and the index survives a faction switch. Also added `test_render.py`, which drives the genuine `ui.lua` tab rendering with a recording window - the previous GUI tests stubbed the menu entirely, which is why three rounds of fixes never touched this. Note: `grind/paths/` had again been overwritten with copies of the ally160 route files, and this time it was carried forward from 1.9.0 into 1.9.1, 1.9.2 and 1.9.3 unnoticed; it shipped 19 duplicate routes in the manifest, 1.90 MB against 1.48 MB. Restored from the previous commit; the routes are untouched in `grind/paths/ally160/`. |
| 2.0.0 | GUI pass. **The '?' marks were non-ASCII characters** - 47 rendered strings contained em dashes, en dashes and middle dots, and the Sylvanas fonts have no glyph for any of them, so each drew as '?'. "Mage - rotation loaded" was literally an em dash. All rendered strings are ASCII now; comments keep their typography since they are never drawn. **The version was invisible because `header_text` is always set** by `gui.lua`, which makes the subtitle branch in `ui.lua` that appended it unreachable. It is now drawn explicitly at the top-left of the header, before anything else, and the title block reserves its line so the gap above the title is even. **The popup close cross was being painted over**: the core draws it top-right before the begin callback runs, and the popup then filled a header bar across the full width. The bar now stops short of the cross bounds and the title centres in what is left. **The grind profile picker is a popup window**, not a dropdown: a dropdown shows 18 rows in a 30px field with no room for the level range or vendor flag, which are what you choose on. The popup lists every route with all three on one row, its height is computed from the catalog so all 27 fit without scrolling, and clicking a row loads it and closes the window. The Grinding tab now shows the chosen route on a button that opens the picker. **Alignment**: the tab draws from one `LEFT`/`LINE` grid instead of ad-hoc offsets, which is what produced the uneven blank space. |
| 2.0.1 | **The running plugin was v1.3.38 the whole time.** `plugin_loader/main.lua` held a hardcoded commit SHA, and it had never been re-uploaded - so the loader kept fetching a commit 29 behind while every new version sat in the repository unseen. Every "nothing has changed" in this session was that. The loader now resolves the branch head itself: one request to `api.github.com/repos/<repo>/commits/<branch>` with `Accept: application/vnd.github.sha`, which answers with the bare 40-character commit id, and every file is then fetched from that immutable commit URL. That keeps the reason it was pinned - raw.githubusercontent caches branch paths unevenly, so fetching by branch can serve a fresh manifest beside a stale module - while removing the need to ever touch the loader again. If the lookup fails it falls back to a pinned commit and says so rather than loading nothing. **Every route dropdown is gone.** One `draw_route_button` is used by the Grinding tab, the Mode tab and the Load Profile popup, so there is exactly one way to pick a profile and the three can no longer disagree about what is selected. The picker popup remains the only place the full list lives, sized from the catalog so all 27 fit. A dropdown was the wrong widget for 27 entries: it caps at 18 rows inside a 30px field and has nowhere to put the level range or the vendor flag. |
| 2.1.0 | The **Class** tab is now **Spells**. Only the label changed: 27 control registrations across the nine rotations and `racials.lua` carry `tab = "class"`, and `ui.lua` special-cases that id for its layout, so renaming the id would mean touching all of them to change one visible word. **The scanner already read the whole spellbook and threw most of it away.** `run_scan` built `book_ids`/`book_names` for every spell the character has, used them to resolve the defined keys, and exposed none of it. It now also groups the whole book into rank families - keyed on `get_base_spell_id`, which is how the client itself says "these are the same spell", falling back to the name when the base id is 0 - and exposes them through `all_families`, `family_count`, `family(name)` and `counts()`. Eleven Frostbolts collapse to one row at the best rank. The Spells tab reports what the scan actually found (`N spells known, M ranks in the spellbook`) instead of a promise that only known spells are listed, and a button opens an **All Known Spells** popup listing every family with its rank count and best-rank id, marking the ones the loaded rotation drives. The class tab's header reserve went from 72px to 104px to fit the button, which would otherwise have been clipped with the first toggle drawn over it. The pasted reference scanner was compared against the shipped one first: same helpers, same timings, same API, `defs` already empty - it was the current file, so there was nothing to port, only the missing accessor to add. |
| 2.2.0 | **The scan was losing most of the spellbook.** `extract_ids` probed twelve entries, decided globally whether ids lived in the keys or the values, and then looked one level deep. Measured against a stub: a flat list worked, a mixed table lost half, and a table nested by spell tab lost all of it - 2 of 5 shapes handled. It is recursive now, taking numeric keys and values at every depth, cycle-guarded. The recursion alone was too greedy - a flat list of four spells has keys 1..4, which became four phantom entries - so every candidate is put to the client (`get_spell_name`, `has_spell`, `is_spell_learned`, `is_spell_known`) and kept only if it answers. An id with no name that the client confirms is kept, which was the other half of the loss. All 5 shapes now return exactly the real spells. **Categories**: new `data/spell_categories.lua` files every spell as Buff, Single Target, Area of Effect, Interrupt, Utility, Racial or Other - explicit ids first (interrupts, where a keyword would get it wrong), then racials from `data/racials.lua`, then keyword rules, and anything left is still shown rather than hidden. The keyword rules are a heuristic and say so; a wrong one is corrected by adding the id, not by making the keywords cleverer. The Spells popup now draws one section per category. **Buff upkeep**: new `buffs.lua` keeps ticked buffs up whenever the bot is working - rotation only, or a grind or quest profile started - and is **not** resting, because a cast breaks eating and drinking and the drink is worth more than an early refresh. Expiry is decided by asking whether the aura is on the player rather than by timing the cast, so a dispel is noticed and no per-rank duration table is needed. One buff per tick with a gap, and a failed cast backs off for six seconds instead of retrying every frame. Toggles live in a Lua table, not menu elements, because the spell list is discovered at runtime and the elements are registered at load - the mismatch that made the route index unreachable in 1.9.3. |
| 2.19.0 | **Questing is driven entirely by RestedXP** (`core.addons.rested_xp`): quest, NPC, waypoint, mob and objective data all come from the addon, and the per-race `quest/data/*.lua` catalog is deleted - every TBC race can quest. `.complete` goals are resolved by the quest's RestedXP objective type (monster -> kill, item -> collect, object -> click). Each goal walks its own step waypoints; the NPC is found by learned quest id, waypoint title, player target, then nearest friendly at the waypoint; the NPC that took an accept/turnin is learned per quest. **The addon read was failing silently**: every result was type-checked as a table, and the core can hand out userdata, so RestedXP looked unloaded. All reads now go through one normalising layer. GUI: all quest settings are on the Questing tab, with a RestedXP Detection panel; the Mode tab is removed. `modes.lua` lists all ten TBC races. New `make_local.py` builds a versioned local test folder. |
| 2.20.0 | **Questing crashed the game on start.** Waypoints were converted with `coords_helper:map_to_world`, which is not a pure conversion: it runs a terrain raycast at the target x,y from the player's height. It ran for every step waypoint on every frame - points that can be across the zone on unloaded terrain - and was handed a plain `{x, y}` table where the API declares a `vec2`. It was the one native call that started running every frame when questing began and never in grind mode. Waypoints now convert through `core.game_ui.get_world_pos_from_map_pos` (no raycast) with a real `vec2`, once per waypoint, cached; terrain height is only queried within 120 yards of the player. `movement/util.lua` `ground_z` likewise skips the native height query for points over 150 yards away (grind legs are shorter, so grind is unaffected). **New error log**: `errorlog.lua` writes one file per session to `scripts_log/MASTER_FARMER_ERRORS/`, appending every line immediately so a crash leaves its trail on disk. `on_update`, `on_render` and the GUI draw run under `errorlog.guard`: a Lua error is logged once with its traceback and a snapshot (mode, status, position, map, guide goal), then counted. The quest engine writes breadcrumbs for each goal, walk destination, engage and NPC interaction. |
| 2.21.0 | **Questing still crashed, mid-fight, on the frame RestedXP rebuilt its step waypoints** (session log: engage, new waypoint, then nothing). A single quest tick was reading the addon dozens of times - `classify` fetched `get_objectives` on every call and was called several times a tick; waypoints, the step and the loaded flag were re-read per lookup; the Questing tab repeated all of it every rendered frame - each read building fresh tables from native data. **`quest/guide.lua` now reads RestedXP once per 0.25 s window** (`refresh`): loaded, step, stickies, current and step waypoints, objectives per quest; the current goal, its kind, targets, waypoints and the GUI snapshot are memoised for the window. The detection panel reads at most once a second. **Memory**: that removes the per-frame table churn; the guide's four separate `get_visible_objects` scans now share `targeting`'s per-frame cache (`targeting.visible_objects`); `on_update` runs a small incremental `collectgarbage("step")`; the error log writes a `MEM` line (Lua heap and peak) every 30 s. Quest mobs tapped by another player are skipped. The error log's `t=` column was divided by 1000 (`core.time()` is already seconds), and every status-line change is now a breadcrumb. Still carried in memory: the loader compiles and keeps every manifest file, so the 27 grind routes (523 KB source) are resident even while questing - see NEXT_FEATURES. |
| 2.22.0 | **Questing still crashed about a second after the first engage** (2.21.0 log: engage Rabbit, status set by `fight_unit`, then nothing - both crash logs end right as the character enters combat). Mitigation: `quest/guide.lua` makes **no RestedXP reads while the player is in combat** and keeps the last snapshot; combat is when the game UI, RestedXP included, is busiest processing events, and the bot needs nothing new from the guide mid-fight. Diagnosis: a **flight recorder** in `errorlog.lua` - starting a mode or engaging a mob arms a burst of 2,500 `PROBE` lines written straight to disk, one at the entry to each native-call stage: every step of the main update cascade, gui.draw and on_render, every RestedXP call by name, each quest-tick phase, and each fight step (set target, auto attack, combat_engage, face, combat_scan, rotation.tick). If it still crashes, the last PROBE line names the call. Also: the camp-mob fallback no longer picks critters, pets, totems or gas clouds (`get_creature_type`), which is how it engaged a Rabbit for Tough Wolf Meat. |
| 2.23.0 | **Travelling to a mob drove Lua memory to its limit.** The out-of-combat pull-in in `movement/combat.lua` (mob over 30 yards away, straight line blocked) issued a Sentinel `move_to` while bypassing every gate navigation has - move gap, failure cooldown, blacklisted areas. When Sentinel answered at once (off-mesh, unreachable, already there) the next frame asked again: a navmesh path request per frame for as long as the bot travelled to the mob, and Sentinel's client shares this Lua state. Quest mode hit it far more than grind because the camp fallback could pick a mob 90 yards off across broken ground. Fixes: the pull-in now respects the move gap, waits `PULL_RETRY` (3 s) after a leg before trying the same mob again, never targets a blacklisted spot, and marks the mob unreachable after `PULL_MAX_TRIES` (3) legs. `sentinel.lua` `N.move` is rate limited to one request per `SN_MIN_GAP` (1 s) whoever calls it; refused requests return false, which every caller already treats as "walk instead". The camp fallback only picks mobs within 40 yards of the player. Evidence for the next log: MEM lines report Sentinel requests issued and rate-limited, and PROBE lines carry the heap size. Grind uses the same combat movement and gets the same fixes. |
| 2.24.0 | **The crash is gone** - the 20:04 session (still on 2.22.0) ran five fights and five minutes with a flat 13-15 MB heap, so keeping RestedXP reads out of combat (2.22.0) was the fix. **But collect quests never progressed**: RestedXP names the item (Tough Wolf Meat), not what drops it, so no unit matched and the camp fallback fought Rockjaw Troggs and Small Crag Boars while the objective sat at 3/8. Collect goals now look first for a mob whose name shares a meaningful word with the item (`guide.find_source_mob`: Tough WOLF Meat -> Ragged Young WOLF; generic words such as meat, pelt, fang, scraps are ignored, plurals folded). When the item names a dropper but none is in sight the bot walks the goal's waypoints, and only after 30 s of that (`SOURCE_PATIENCE`) does the camp fallback get a turn; items whose names give no clue still use the camp fallback. The flight recorder now arms once per Start (first fight) instead of every engage - 15,000 lines in five minutes. |
| 2.25.0 | **Quest mode never looted.** Three causes: (1) `targeting.find_corpses` used `izi.enemies_if`, which lists enemies - a corpse is not one - so the scan came back empty; the only other path was the kill target, which the quest engine clears when the mob dies. Corpses now come from the visible-object list (dead, lootable, in range), `enemies_if` kept as a fallback. (2) The quest engine pulled the next mob at once; combat movement took the player and loot's walk to the corpse was refused. New `loot.has_work()`: while a corpse of ours is waiting, the quest engine stops its own walk once and waits (fighting back still comes first). (3) Navigation answers "already moving" as success whatever the destination, so loot could not redirect a walk in progress - hence the one-time stop. Corpses whose loot attempts are used up are never picked, so one bad corpse cannot stall the bot. **300-yard ceiling everywhere**: `targeting.MAX_RANGE = 300` clamps every finder in targeting.lua (find_mobs, find_named, find_npc, find_corpses, combat_scan, threat_nearby, and the internal scans) and quest/guide.lua's five; the 1000-yard "target too far" checks in both engines are now 300; no navigation leg (`nav.lua` `clamp_leg`) or Sentinel path request (`N.move`) spans more than `K.MAX_LEG` = 300 yards - a far waypoint is reached in legs re-planned as each lands. |
| 2.26.0 | **Melee closes to 2 yards.** Every melee rotation reports a 5-yard combat range, and combat movement treated 5 yards from the target's centre as "in position" (CHASE_BAND takes 5 below 5, so it fell back to 5); `S.approach_unit` floored the approach at 5 yards and `combat_hop` refused hops under `MIN_NAV`, so a melee fighter could stand out of swing range of small mobs and never close the last yards. Now a combat range <= `MELEE_YARDS` (5) is melee: in position only within `MELEE_REACH` (2 yd) with line of sight, no hysteresis past it, approach aims at `MELEE_STANDOFF` (1 yd) so the walker's 1-yard arrival tolerance still lands inside 2, a direct step-in when the target is within one hop and the line is clear, and final hops down to `MELEE_MIN_HOP` (1 yd, the walker's own threshold - shorter moves could be refused and blacklist the ground beside the mob). New `rotation.is_melee`; warrior, rogue and paladin declare it, shaman with Enhancement on, druid in cat form (no GUI control yet, so druid stays a caster), and a melee rotation always reports 5 yards. No melee profile retreats, so nothing fights the rule. Applies to grind, quest, fight-back and the mage's engage alike, since all go through `combat_engage`. |
| 2.27.0 | **Melee never reached its target** (20:25 log: 8+ s of "closing" on the first wolf). 2.26.0 aimed 1 yard short of the target, and the walker treats a destination within its 1-yard final threshold as already reached - from 2-2.5 yards the hop "arrived" without moving, the range never fell to 2, and the same hop was re-issued every half second. Melee now aims at the target's own position (`MELEE_STANDOFF` 0): every hop is at least 2 yards and the walker stops within 1. **GUI garbage**: the recorder's heap column showed the GUI draw allocating ~83 KB a frame, 60% of everything allocated; the 59 `color.new` calls in gui.lua (mostly inside draw functions, every frame) now go through a cache that builds each colour once, and `gui.draw` checks `is_started` once per frame instead of three times. The loot corpse pick is shared for 0.2 s between `loot.tick` and `loot.has_work`, halving the per-frame object scans in fights. **The 20:25 crash** came ~15 s after the 2,500-line recorder burst ran out, so the recorder is now time-based - 90 s from Start, bounded only by MAX_LINES - with finer probes at the head of each update (`izi.on_update`, keybinds) and inside the GUI draw (sync_player, menu:draw, sync_attack_level). |
| 2.28.0 | **CPU, not memory.** The Plugin Monitor showed 14 MB of Lua memory (healthy) but 93 ms of Lua time a frame, 99% of it Master Farmer; the 20:30 session ran ~17 fps with the recorder on and did not crash. Every frame ran the whole decision cascade - death, conjure, healing, loot scan, buffs, trainer, vendor, equip, the mode tick with target scans, combat movement and the class rotation. **Decisions now run at `BOT_TICK` = 10 Hz**; the stale check, izi's update, keybinds and `movement.pulse` (which drives the walker between decisions) stay per frame, as does the GUI. `movement/fsm.lua` keeps a combat request live for `COMBAT_REQ_TTL` (0.35 s) instead of clearing it every frame, so frames between two bot ticks do not read as "combat stopped". **The flight recorder is opt-in** - it wrote ~20 disk lines a frame and only arms when "Log quest and guide steps" is ticked. **Profiler**: every probe point now adds its `core.cpu_time()` cost to an in-memory tally (no disk), and a `PERF` line every 10 s reports the plugin's CPU ms per frame and the eight most expensive stages - `core.time()` only advances once a frame, so the recorder's timestamps could not split a frame. |
| 2.29.0 | **The frame drop was the flight recorder.** The 2.28.0 profiler showed 1.28 ms/frame idle (~300 fps) and 68-95 ms/frame after Start, spread evenly at ~3 ms over EVERY probe point - the cost of the probe itself: "Log quest and guide steps" was ticked, which armed the recorder, and each recorder line is a ~2.7 ms disk write. The Plugin Monitor agrees: 0.29 ms idle. The recorder is now its own opt-in box, "Crash recorder (slows the game)", off by default; "Log quest and guide steps" no longer arms it. **Memory - plugin_loader 1.2.0** (installed copy and repo updated together; the 1.1.0 copy is backed up beside the scripts folder): the loader kept every downloaded source string (~1.6 MB) and a duplicate chunk table for the whole session. New `http_loader.release()` drops them after hand-off, called from `plugin_loader/main.lua` once `main` is required; older http_loader copies without it are skipped. |
| 2.30.0 | **Loader 1.2.0 confirmed**: heap at load fell from 16.6 MB to 10.8 MB, and questing ran at 0.29-0.37 ms/frame (~540 fps). **But the game crashed ~4 s after the second engage** - and the pattern across sessions is frame rate: it crashed at full speed (2.20, 2.21, 2.29) and never while the flight recorder held it near 10 fps (2.22, 2.27). The Questing tab asked for the guide snapshot from the RENDER callback, and once the 0.25 s window lapsed that read RestedXP inside the render hook - potentially hundreds of times a second - as could the objectives lookup and the detection panel. **RestedXP is now read only inside the update callback**: `ns_call` refuses unless main.lua has opened `guide.allow_reads` around `on_update`; outside it `refresh` and `objectives` keep the last snapshot, the detection panel files a request the next update fills, and `guide.update()` keeps the snapshot current every bot tick while Questing is enabled (started or not). Derived values computed outside the window (memo, objective names, a goal's kind) are returned but never cached, so the engine never reuses an answer that lacked addon data. |
| 2.31.0 | **Corpses were still not looted.** (1) `is_lootable` required both `can_be_looted` and `has_loot`; `has_loot` (does it hold items) is not reliably populated before a loot window opens, so corpses were rejected. `can_be_looted` - the lootable flag, set only for whoever holds loot rights - is now the test, with `has_loot` only on a build lacking it. (2) "Loot My Kills Only" needs the kill recorded, but `fight_unit` (quest and grind) tested validity before death, so a corpse whose object reported invalid at death was released unrecorded and refused forever. Death is now checked first, and a lootable corpse that is not tap-denied counts as ours. **Crash** now survives ~95 s at full frame rate (was seconds), CPU 0.3-0.5 ms/frame. The full recorder slows the game enough to hide it, so a **heartbeat** replaces it for everyday runs: one `BEAT` line per bot tick (10/s, ~0.05 ms/frame average) listing the stages that tick ran through plus status, HP, combat and the target with its distance - the last BEAT before a crash is the last complete tick. `loot:fire` and `killed` are marked too. |
| 2.32.0 | **Crash audit.** The 20:52 crash came while walking up to the next mob right after a kill, smooth frames and flat memory. Fixed: (1) **stale object handles** - the kill target is held across ticks, and once the game frees the object behind it any method but `is_valid` is a native read of freed memory that `pcall` cannot catch. 2.31.0 put `is_dead` before `is_valid` in quest and grind `fight_unit`; the loot fast path, the heartbeat (`get_name`/`distance_to` every tick), the rotation's dead-target check and the quest item-use path touched the stored target unchecked. All now test `is_valid` first; an engaged target gone invalid is recorded as a kill from its saved GUID. The shared object list is cached one bot tick (0.1 s) instead of 0.5 s. (2) **Spellbook** re-scanned the whole book - several native calls per spell - every 2 s; now on level change, after a trainer purchase (`spellbook.request_rescan`), or every 60 s. (3) **set_target** was issued up to 3x a tick on an unchanged target; `targeting.ensure_target` sets it only when it differs. (4) **Auto-attack** restarted every tick, including from 10+ yards on the approach; now within 6 yards and at most once a second per target. (5) **Two controllers turning the character**: the walker runs look_at every frame while moving, and `Rg.face` added a look_at lock on top; facing now stands down while the walker or Sentinel is moving. (6) **Two target selectors**: `combat.acquire` inside `rotation.tick` picked and `set_current`-ed a new target whenever an engine handed it a dead one; with an engine in charge (`ctx.no_move`) the rotation now stops instead. (7) The heartbeat's stage list was always empty - its state was declared below `errorlog.probe`, so inside probe it was global nil. |
| 2.33.0 | **No crash**: the 21:01 session ran 5.5+ minutes (stopped by hand), heap 11.8-14.1 MB, 0.4 ms/frame - the 2.32.0 audit held. **Looting rebuilt** (`loot.lua`). The heartbeat showed the old design failing in one tick: `loot.tick` ran before the engine noticed the death and cached "no corpse", the engine's `has_work` read that stale answer and pulled the next mob, and once combat movement owned the player the walk to the corpse was refused. Now: engines call `loot.note_kill(unit)` in the kill tick (quest, grind and path mode), queuing the corpse GUID and position, so `has_work` is true before the next pull is considered; `loot.tick` resolves each GUID fresh with `core.object_manager.get_object_from_guid` every tick (no handle is kept - the API's own rule), walks to within 3.5 yd, releases combat movement if the fight is over, and calls `core.input.loot_object(corpse, true)` (auto loot) at most once a second, three times; done when the corpse stops being lootable, with any open loot window closed via `core.input.close_loot`. Attackers are fought first; 15 s per corpse, 60 s queue memory. A 1 s fallback scan queues lootable corpses within 15 yd. GUI: **"Auto Loot Corpses"** on the General tab turns all of it on or off; "Loot My Kills Only" is gone (can_be_looted already means loot rights). Noted, not changed: `equip.tick` claims ticks for 1-2 s at a time retrying the same glove equip. |
| 2.34.0 | **Auto-equip retry loop, and the vendor selling the wrong items.** The API documents that the slot index from `core.inventory.get_items_in_bag` is shifted by one from what `core.input.use_container_item` takes - "passing a raw slot_id straight from get_items_in_bag targets the item NEXT to the one you meant". `equip.lua` and `vendor.lua` both passed it raw: auto-equip used the item beside the upgrade, so the gloves never moved and were retried every 10 s, each attempt holding the whole decision cascade for 1.5 s mid-approach; the vendor sold the item beside each grey/white. New `bags.lua` lists bag items through `inventory_helper:get_character_bag_slots()` minus equipped items and uses `use_container_item(bag_id, bag_slot)` - the pattern the working SP / SP_Pull plugins use. Auto-equip now: every candidate passes the full `equip.usable_by` (armour weight, weapon type, shield, relic, required level) instead of armour weight alone; the armour cap is level-aware (Warrior/Paladin mail below 40 and plate from 40, Hunter/Shaman leather below 40 and mail from 40); each attempt is verified 1.5 s later by checking the item left its bag slot, and after 2 failures the item is skipped for the session with a log line; the cascade is no longer held while an attempt lands (only a bind-on-equip prompt holds it). |
| 2.35.0 | **Looting confirmed working** (21:20 log: Tough Wolf Meat 4/8 -> 5/8 after "looting before the next pull"). **Crash on a shield / two-hander swap.** The last action before the crash was "Equipping Bent Large Shield (empty slot)" while closing on a wolf at 2.8 yd. Auto-equip judged each slot on its own: a shield unequips a worn two-hander, the next scan then saw an empty main hand and equipped the two-hander, which unequips the shield - and it did it mid-engage, because the only guard was "in combat" and at 2.8 yd the character was not flagged yet while the rotation was already starting auto-attack. Now: no equip while there is a live kill target, while combat movement owns the player, or while a corpse is waiting to be looted; a shield / off-hand / held item is never equipped over a worn two-hander, and a two-hander never while the off-hand is occupied; after any main-hand / off-hand / ranged equip no other weapon-slot equip for 30 s. |
| 2.36.0 | **Paladin: Seal of Righteousness (21084 and ranks) only in combat.** It was pre-cast out of combat from `paladin.buffs_ooc`, and `paladin.tick` - which also runs while closing on a mob - put it up on the walk in. The out-of-combat pre-cast is removed, and the in-fight upkeep now requires `player:is_in_combat()`. The Judgement sequence still waits for the seal aura, so the first Judgement follows the first in-combat seal. |
| 2.37.0 | **S-shaped approach to a mob.** Outside the last few yards combat movement closed the gap in `STEER_HOP` (5 yd) hops picked by the steering search from angled offsets and side-steps, re-planned about every half second from wherever the last hop ended - a chain of slightly angled hops, weaving. New **direct chase** in `combat_engage`: when the straight line to the target is clear (`walk_open`), one move straight at it - to melee contact, or to the class's range - re-aimed only when the target shifts more than `CHASE_REISSUE` (2 yd) from where the move is headed; it runs before the "already moving" wait so a sidestepping mob is followed promptly. Steering remains the fallback when the line is blocked; the path leash still applies. **Always attack what is attacking you**: quest and grind only fought back with no target, so a bot chasing one mob ignored another hitting it. New `targeting.attacker_to_switch` (compared by GUID, never by touching the stored target) switches to the nearest attacker whenever the current target is not one of them; path mode already re-picks attackers first every tick. |
| 2.38.0 | **Always fight back.** The quest tick returned early - RestedXP not loaded, no active step, step complete, resting - before it ever reached `fight_back`, and the first two released combat movement every tick; grind with no target only looked for attackers when its periodic scan came round. `fight_back` is now the first thing in the quest tick and the grind engine checks `attacker_to_switch` every tick, both ahead of the rest check; a RestedXP step change mid-fight no longer resets the target. **Corpses not always looted.** The lootable flag is set a moment after death, and a corpse queued in the kill tick was dropped on the next as "never ours" while `can_be_looted` was still false - now a 3 s grace (`FLAG_GRACE`) before a not-yet-lootable corpse is dropped, walking over but not firing until the flag appears. A target that went invalid at death is queued from its saved GUID and position (`loot.note_kill_guid`). Fallback scan widened to 30 yd (casters kill at range); queue memory 120 s. |
| 2.39.0 | **Combat lock: kill every attacker before the waypoint.** Attackers were only mobs targeting the player, looked for out to combat range + 10 yd (15 for melee), so a caster at 25 yd, a mob on the pet, or one that dropped its target for a moment (casting, fleeing, feared) did not count, and the bot walked back to its waypoint mid-fight. New `targeting.threats`: every live, attackable mob in combat targeting the player OR the pet within `THREAT_RANGE` (40 yd), minus mobs already marked unreachable. The quest `fight_back` is now a lock: switch to any threat that is not the current target; keep fighting the current target while it lives; engage any remaining threat; and while the player is still in combat with nothing in view, hold position (`targeting.combat_hold`, up to 8 s) instead of walking off. Grind uses the same threats and hold. **Every kill looted**: loot waits on the same threats (a mob on the pet included) and its fallback scan covers the same 40 yd, so every corpse from a fight is in range once it ends; the hold window leaves time for it. |
| 2.40.0 | **Seal of Righteousness (21084) cast constantly.** Two causes. (1) `auras.lua` asked the SDK `buff_manager` first and treated ANY answer as final - "not active" for ids it does not track included - so the direct `unit:has_buff` / `has_aura` checks never ran and a seal that was up read as missing, re-cast every global. A manager "not active" is now confirmed against the unit before it is believed (buff, debuff and aura queries; helps every class's buff upkeep). (2) "in combat" is not "attacking": the seal now needs the player in combat AND a live, attackable target within 8 yd (`seal_attacking`), plus a 3 s latch after each cast for the aura to be reported. In TBC Judgement consumes the seal, so it is still re-applied after each Judgement (~every 8-10 s) - the normal Retribution cycle. |
| 2.41.0 | **Resting while questing.** `resting.tick` cancelled a rest outright whenever anything attackable stood within 10 yd - critters and neutral mobs included - and the tick fell through to the quest engine, which pulled the next mob at low health; quest objectives sit in camps, so there nearly always was something close. Now only a real threat counts (`is_enemy_with` the player or already in combat, not a critter), the check runs only once a rest is actually needed, and instead of giving up the bot walks 20 yd straight away from the threat and rests there, holding the tick so nothing else can start a fight; after 12 s without a clear spot it rests where it stands. The Healing tab's "Eat Below HP %" / "Drink Below Mana %" sliders now set the thresholds - every rotation hard-coded 30% - with a rotation's `drink_pct = 0` (no mana) respected. |
| 2.42.0 | **Questing stayed interacting with the merchant after selling and repairing.** (1) The quest engine's talk goal (RestedXP `.vendor` / `.trainer` / `.fp` / gossip steps) re-interacted every 1.5 s unless a GOSSIP frame was open - a merchant window is not one - and RestedXP does not tick every such step off itself, so it never ended. Now once any NPC frame is open (gossip, merchant, trainer) and no vendor trip is running, the goal counts as done after 2 s (`guide.mark_goal_done`, keyed by step and goal, forgotten when the step changes) and the bot moves on. (2) `vendor.tick` ignored a merchant window it had not opened itself and bailed with "No merchant for this zone" before selling; a merchant window opened by a quest step or the player is now a trip right there (sell, restock, repair), checked before the zone merchant lookup, with a 60 s cooldown so the same window is not worked twice. (3) `finish_trip` now releases the merchant target (kind "vendor") it set before interacting. There is no API to close a merchant window; walking out of range closes it, which the bot now does. |
| 2.43.0 | **Trainer hung the bot after training was complete.** With nothing left to buy, `trainer.tick` reset its 0.6 s gap and returned false once - but the trainer window stays open (no API closes it), and during every following gap it returned `service_count() > 0`, i.e. true: it claimed ~95% of all ticks, so nothing walked the bot away, which is the only thing that closes the window. Now a `finished` flag makes it return false for as long as the finished window stays open (cleared when it closes), and the gap only holds the cascade mid-purchase. `cheapest_affordable` also "bought" already-learned and too-high-level services once per visit (0.6 s each): services whose `category` is present and not "available" (used / unavailable / header) are skipped. A 30-purchase cap per visit is a hard stop, and "Training complete" is logged once. |
| 2.44.0 | **NPC-stuck watchdog** (`watchdog.lua`). Asked for a forced game reload after 5 minutes stuck at an NPC - the Sylvanas API has no UI reload, client restart, logout, macro, console or plugin-reload call, so the watchdog does what a reload would do for the bot instead. "Stuck" is 5 minutes continuously at an NPC (gossip / merchant / trainer / quest window open, a vendor trip running, or the quest engine on an accept / turn in / talk goal - `quest.in_npc_interaction`), gaps under 10 s forgiven. Recovery: close the gossip, quest and loot frames, clear the target, reset vendor / trainer / loot / quest-dialog state, count the current guide goal done (`quest.skip_current_goal`), walk 20 yd away (which also closes a merchant or trainer window). Three recoveries within 15 minutes stop the bot (`gui.stop`) with a log line instead of looping. Quest modules are only consulted when already loaded, so grind mode does not load the quest pack. |
| 2.45.0 | **Stuck travelling -> Hearthstone.** `watchdog.lua` now also watches travel: the quest engine walking to a waypoint (`quest.in_travel`) or navigation moving the character, for 5 minutes without getting 20 yd from where the stretch began (fights, rests, loot and NPC visits restart the clock). It stops, uses Hearthstone 6948 (`izi.item:use_self`) and holds every tick for 12 s so nothing moves the character and cancels the 10 s cast; the guide re-routes from home. With the stone on cooldown or missing it logs why, walks off 20 yd to try another line, and starts again. **Seal of Righteousness cast as 21084 only** - the rotation cast the highest known rank of all nine; the "already up" check still recognises every rank. **Melee 3-yard standoff**: aiming at the target's own position (2.27.0) ran the character into and through it, and it overshot and circled, re-chasing each time the gap opened past 2 yd. Melee now aims 3 yd short (`MELEE_STANDOFF`), is in position within 4 yd (`MELEE_REACH`) and only chases again past 5 yd (`MELEE_HOLD`) - still swing range. |
| 2.46.0 | **Kills still not looted.** `loot.lua` resolved each queued corpse with `core.object_manager.get_object_from_guid`, which (per its documentation) hands the string to the client's UNIT-TOKEN resolver - "target", "nameplate7" - and a corpse has no token (no nameplate, target already cleared), so it came back nil and every corpse was dropped as "gone" on the next tick. Corpses are now resolved by searching the visible-object list (targeting's per-tick cache) for the GUID; the token resolver is only tried first and only trusted when its object carries the same GUID. The bot's own kills always get at least one loot attempt whatever `can_be_looted` says (the flag decides scan-found corpses and "already looted"). A loot window auto loot left items in is emptied slot by slot (`core.input.loot_item`) and closed. **Loot before any other action**: `loot.tick` moved to straight after `death` in the cascade - ahead of conjure, healing/eating, buffs, trainer, vendor, equip and the mode engine - and lost its resting gate (loot first, eat after); it still steps aside for attackers. Every corpse queued, dropped (with the reason) and attempted is now a TRAIL line in the session log. |
| 2.47.0 | **Looting was switched off by full bags.** The 22:54 log shows every kill queued ("loot: queued ... our kill") and never attempted, with the status "Vendor: No merchant for this zone": the bags were full, and `loot.tick` / `loot.has_work` returned early on `bags_too_full()`. The gate is gone - stackables, quest items (Wendigo Mane) and gold still loot into full bags, and a failed attempt costs nothing. **Full bags, no known merchant -> Hearthstone.** Zones without merchant data left full bags full for good; now the vendor uses Hearthstone 6948 (held still for the cast; loot yields to it), then finds a merchant at the inn by talking to the nearest friendly NPCs (selecting the vendor gossip option) until a merchant window opens, where the merchant-window trip sells, restocks and repairs. Hearthstone not ready: logged, carry on. **"Invalid game object!" x150**: `combat_engage` compared `R.combat_target ~= unit`, and object `__eq` throws once the stored object is freed, aborting combat movement every tick; targets are compared by GUID now (`R.combat_guid`), as are the player checks in the fsm combat-event handlers. |
| 2.48.0 | **Attacked mid-rest, the bot could not fight back.** The 23:02 log shows a whole Wendigo fight "closing" at 1-3 yd, HP 97 -> dead. A rest set `resting` and the movement rest lock; in combat `healing.tick` only did potions and never reached `resting.tick`, the one place that cleared them - so `combat_engage` returned at its rest-lock check and `rotation.tick` returned while "resting", for the whole fight. Combat now ends any rest (`resting.clear()` in the in-combat branch). **A kill hidden by the cascade was never looted**: kills were detected inside the quest/grind tick, which runs last, and a rest starting as combat ended claimed the tick first - the Crag Boar was later found by the scan as "not ours" and dropped after 0 attempts. `loot.tick` (first after death) now watches the kill target itself every tick and queues its corpse as ours when it dies or goes invalid. Every mob targeted to kill is remembered by GUID for 5 minutes (`state.note_engaged`), and the fallback scan queues those corpses as ours - a loot attempt whatever the lootable flag says. |
| 2.49.0 | **Low durability -> Hearthstone and repair.** When gear is broken or below the "repair %" slider, out of combat, the vendor now stops questing and uses Hearthstone 6948 first - even when a zone merchant is known - then searches the inn for a merchant that CAN repair: a merchant window without `can_merchant_repair()` (an innkeeper) is skipped and the search goes on, over 60 yd and up to 12 NPCs for a repair trip, so the smiths near the inn are reached. The vendor claims the cascade for the whole trip, so questing and other movement wait until the repair is done. A Hearthstone that is not ready falls back to walking to the zone merchant when one is known. |
| 2.50.0 | **Quest NPC interaction audit.** (1) `loot.tick` threw "attempt to index local 'e' (a nil value)" (23:02 and 23:30 logs) - it kept the best corpse's INDEX while dropping lower entries in the same backward pass, and every drop shifted it - aborting the whole cascade (quest, NPCs, healing) every tick while it repeated; the entry itself is kept now. (2) RestedXP guide text carries colour and texture codes (`|cFF...`, `|cRXP_..._`, `|r`, `|T..|t`, `|H..|h`); the quest title matched against an NPC's quest list and the names matched against mobs came from it, so titles never matched except by the lone-entry shortcut. `clean_text` strips them from goal, objective and waypoint text. (3) Turn-in: `complete_quest()` is the PROGRESS window's "Continue", but the state machine went straight to verify - the completion window was never finished, the quest stayed in the log, the bot re-interacted every 8 s and gave up after three tries. A finish stage now takes the best reward choice or `get_quest_reward(0)` ("Complete Quest", no choice). (4) A wrong NPC (a guard picked as nearest at the waypoint) was retried until the tries ran out; `npc.accept` / `npc.turn_in` now report "not_offered" (quest not in its list) or "gave_up", and the engine rules that NPC out for the goal and moves to the next candidate. |
| 2.51.0 | **Kill mobs on the way to quest waypoints.** Quest mode only fought when attacked or when a goal asked for a kill, and walked past everything else - leaving mobs to aggro from behind. Every walk the engine makes goes through `walk_to`; before each leg, at most every 0.8 s, `guide.find_path_mob` looks for a hostile (`is_enemy_with` the player, or in combat), fightable, non-critter mob within 20 yd, within 70 degrees of the direction of travel (or within 5 yd whatever the angle), from 4 levels below to 3 above the player, and the engine fights it through the normal path - combat lock, loot, then on. Skipped under 50% health (the rest comes first). Questing tab checkbox "Kill mobs on the way", on by default. |
| 2.52.0 | **"Spell not learned" spam - every class.** Rotations build each spell from a rank list (highest first) and the spellbook's learned test answered yes when ANY rank was in the book, but the cast went out as the spell object's own id - not necessarily a rank the character had. `spellbook.watch(spell, ids, track_buff, track_debuff)` now returns a thin proxy (rotation code unchanged: `spell:cast_safe(...)` etc. forward to it), and after every scan - start-up, level-up, trainer purchase - each proxy is re-bound to a fresh `izi.spell(best)` for the highest rank of its list actually in the book, with aura tracking re-applied over the whole list. A spell with no known rank reads as not learned and is never cast. All nine rotations pass their rank lists. Seal of Righteousness goes back to its full rank list (2.45.0 forced 21084, which may not be a rank this character has). |
| 2.53.0 | **RestedXP-marked units.** The guide puts raid-target icons (`get_target_marker_index` 1-8, star ... skull) on the NPC a step wants spoken to and the mobs it wants killed; the bot ignored them. New `guide.find_marked(player, range, "friendly"/"hostile", exclude)`: the nearest living marked unit - friendly = not attackable, hostile = fightable (alive, attackable, not tapped by another player). Accept / turn in / talk: a marked friendly NPC is the second choice in `find_giver`, after an NPC learned for that quest and ahead of the waypoint title and nearest-at-waypoint guesses (ruled-out givers skipped). Kill / collect: a marked hostile in range is engaged first, before name matching, the drop-source guess and the camp fallback. Use-item goals with nothing targeted use the item on a marked hostile. |
| 2.54.0 | **Mobs on the way were engaged but never killed.** The newest log shows path clearing firing ("clear the path: Wendigo") and then six Wendigo fights in a minute with no kill: every heartbeat "closing" while the distance GREW 4 -> 14 yd. `combat_engage` returned while any Sentinel leg was running, to let a pull-in land - but the waypoint walk is a Sentinel leg too, so the character kept walking to the quest waypoint with the mob hitting its back. `N.move` now records why a leg was issued (`R.sn_why`); combat only waits for its own "pull" leg and halts any other (waypoint, loot) the moment it engages. **Seals out of combat**: `buffs.lua` maintains every enabled buff-category spell, and "^Seal of " is a buff category, so Seal of Righteousness (21084) and Seal of the Crusader (21082) were cast out of combat as self-buffs, bypassing the paladin rotation's gate. Seals are skipped there now; the rotation casts its seal only while actively attacking an enemy within 8 yd. |
| 2.55.0 | **30 seconds, then move on.** A corpse not looted within 30 s of the kill (`GIVE_UP`) is dropped whatever held it up - the walk, the attempts, a fight in between - and the bot carries on questing; the log says "gave up after 30 s". It is then ignored for 10 minutes so the fallback scan cannot queue it again and restart the wait (it had re-queued a Crag Boar corpse after dropping it). Applied in both `loot.tick` and `loot.has_work`, so nothing keeps waiting on it. |
| 2.56.0 | **Already-looted corpses were worked again.** A finished corpse was not remembered - the 1 s fallback scan found it again (the lootable flag lags, or items that are not ours stay on it), queued it and walked over to "loot" it again ("looted after 2 attempts", then "queued (found nearby)" again in the logs). And the bot's own kills got an attempt whatever the flag said, so a corpse emptied by hand was still walked to. Now every finished corpse - looted, empty, attempts used up, not lootable, not found, gave up - is ignored for 10 minutes; a corpse seen lootable that no longer is counts as "already looted" (no walk, no attempt); `has_loot() == false` after the 3 s flag grace counts as "empty"; after an attempt, un-lootable OR empty counts as looted; and the scan does not queue plainly empty corpses. |
| 2.57.0 | **GUI: the Healing tab is now "Resting"** (label only - the tab id stays `healing`, so saved settings and code references are untouched). Its Eat / Drink sliders are now "Eat when Health below %" and "Drink when Mana below %", range 10-90% (was 20-35%), default 35%, and are shown only while "Eat / Drink" is ticked. New `visible_if` control option in ui.lua (a function; the control is hidden while it does not return true), checked in `Menu:control_visible`. The resting logic already reads these sliders (2.41.0). |
| 2.58.0 | **Sliders never showed in the GUI - any of them.** Native elements draw at the window's dynamic cursor (`add_menu_element_pos_offset` / `begin_group`, as the SDK's UI example does), but ui.lua lays every control out by absolute coordinates and rendered natives inside `begin_window_sub_context` under `pcall`, which failed silently; only the custom-drawn widgets (checkboxes, drop-downs, launchers) ever appeared. New `Menu:draw_slider_row` draws sliders with the same primitives as the checkbox rows - label and value, a bar with 20 click zones (the API has no mouse-position read; clicking anywhere on the bar jumps to that value) and - / + step boxes - writing through the native element's `set()`, so values persist and `gui.slider()` reads them as before. Every slider_int / slider_float in the menu goes through it. |
| 2.59.0 | **Sentinel-first movement** - travel on Sentinel, combat on the local controller, every call feature-detected and falling back to the old behaviour. (1) `N.reachable` - cached `validate_destination` (4 yd grid, 5 min, never blocks: pending/no server = reachable); the quest engine skips waypoints Sentinel reports unreachable and skips a goal whose every waypoint is, with a log line, instead of walking into the 5-minute stuck watchdog. (2) Recorded grind routes: `nav_path` hands the waypoint list to `client:follow_path` (`N.follow`, same gates and 1 s rate limit as `N.move`) for Sentinel's stuck recovery; the walker is the fallback. Grind zones already used `plan_route` + `validate_destination`. (3) No 300-yard clamp for Sentinel legs - `N.move` gets the real destination; `MAX_LEG` now only limits walker legs. (4) Blocked line to a mob mid-fight: `N.chase_path` asks `nav_client.find_path` (cached per target, re-asked when the target moves 5 yd or after 3 s, at most once a second) and the local walker drives the waypoints - not deferred while casting as a Sentinel move is; steering is the fallback while pending. (5) Combat retreat asks `nav_client.flee` (pack) or `kite` (single target) for a mesh-aware spot, fresh for 1.5 s; the geometric away-vector is the fallback. (6) Blacklisted areas are mirrored into `client.obstacle` avoidance zones (re-synced when ours expire), so Sentinel paths around them. |
| 2.60.0 | **Logo not showing.** The asset is present (`scripts_data\mf_assets\mf_assets\logo.png`, 2146x492 PNG) and the header sizes it to 345x79, but `ensure_logo` kept the texture only when `type(tex_id) == "number"` - anything else the native returns was discarded every half second and nothing drew. Any non-nil id is kept now (the pattern FB_Fury uses for its window logo), `draw_texture` falls back to `draw_texture_rect` (full UV, FB_Fury's call), and a read, decode or draw failure is logged once with the reason instead of failing silently. **GUI size**: "Window width" (700-1600) and "Window height" (500-1100) sliders on the Settings tab, applied live; defaults are the old 851x644. |
| 2.61.0 | **Logo still not showing.** `ui.lua`'s logo code is unchanged since 2.2.0, when it drew, so the regression was the runtime: since 2.21.0 `main.lua` runs a garbage-collector step every frame, and the PNG bytes handed to `core.graphics.load_texture` were a temporary string - collected before the texture was uploaded, leaving it blank. The bytes (and the font data) are now kept alive with the texture. Every logo step - read, decode, texture id / size, first draw position - is also written to the `MASTER_FARMER_ERRORS` session log, so if it is still missing the log says which step failed. |
| 2.62.0 | **Keep Gold Reserve removed.** The Settings tab slider (`mfg_train_reserve`), its `train_reserve` alias in `gui.lua` and `trainer.lua`'s `reserve()` are gone; training now spends against the character's full gold, cheapest rank first. |
| 2.63.0 | **Quest NPC interaction.** (1) The dialog state machine now reports how it ended - `done`, `skipped` (grey), `not_offered`, `gave_up` - on every call; it used to return nothing once finished, so the engine could not tell finished from working and stood at the NPC until RestedXP ticked the goal or the 5-minute watchdog fired. A landed dialog now counts the goal done after 1.5 s if RestedXP has not. (2) An accept already in the quest log, or a turn-in already handed in, is counted done before walking to the NPC. (3) An interact with no NPC in reach no longer uses up one of the three tries and reads a frame that never opened. (4) A gossip frame listing no quests but other options (guard, flight master) is `not_offered` at once instead of after three 8-second retries. (5) When every NPC at the waypoint has been ruled out, the list is retried after 20 s instead of only when the goal changes. (6) The walk re-routes to the giver when it is not where the bot is already heading (moving NPCs, NPC off the waypoint). (7) Accept / turn-in goals are labelled by quest title, not the quest's objective line ("Objective Complete"). |
| 2.64.0 | **Spells tab and rotation rebuilt for every class.** The "View all known spells" button, its popup, the mage-buff row and the per-class checkboxes are gone. The Spells tab now lists only the spells the character knows from its class's DPS / healing / tanking catalog (`data/class_spells.lua`, all nine TBC classes) plus its castable racials, grouped by role, each with a tick box; groups that cannot stack (aura, seal, blessing, armor, aspect, stance, form, pet, curse, shield, imbue) use the first ticked spell. New `smart.lua` builds the rotation from the ticked spells: heal -> defensive -> interrupt (target and any caster in the pack) -> racials -> in-combat buffs / seal -> resource -> opener -> execute -> control -> DoTs / debuffs -> totems -> cooldowns -> AoE -> finishers -> damage -> wand / auto shot, best known rank, one cast per call, failed spells set aside briefly. Every ticked buff is kept up in AND out of combat (`rotation.buffs_ooc` -> `smart.upkeep`), pets are summoned / revived / healed out of combat. Wired into Rotation Only (never stops or turns the player; cast-time spells wait until standing), Grinding and Questing (walker held for the cast). Four Spells tab sliders: self-heal %, defensives %, AoE enemy count, wand mana %. Racials now follow their Spells tab tick. The class modules keep supplying range, melee/caster shape, movement profile and rest thresholds; a ticked Cat/Bear form or Stormstrike makes a druid / shaman melee. |
| 2.65.0 | **Crash logging no longer goes blind.** The last game crash (v2.56.0, 00:52) left no trail: `errorlog.lua` stopped writing after 20000 lines, which ten heartbeats a second reach in about half an hour, so every crash later in a long session was invisible. The session now rolls over into `_part2.log`, `_part3.log`... and an unchanged heartbeat is written at most every 2 s (with a `(+N same)` count), cutting the log volume by roughly 20x on an idle or steady bot. `make_local.py` also compiles every file with real Lua (lupa) when available - luaparser had accepted a newline inside a string. |
| 2.66.0 | **Vendor sell loop.** At Godric Rothgar the bot "sold" item 7073 (Broken Fang) 79 times in a row: the sale never happened, the item stayed in its bag slot, and the next pass picked it again, so the trip never reached restock / repair / done. Every sale is now verified on the next pass; an item still in the same bag slot after 2 attempts is skipped for the rest of the trip, and the trip carries on. Each sale and each skipped slot is logged (`vendor:` trail with bag, slot and global slot) to pin down why the client refused it. |
| 2.67.0 | **Resting made visible in the session log.** The 11:48 session (v2.65.0) crashed 0.1 s after acquiring a Young Wolf at 44 yd, out of combat, and the mage had not drunk once in two sessions - but the log carried no mana and resting wrote only to the in-game console, so neither could be traced. Every BEAT now shows `mp`. `resting.lua` writes `rest:` TRAIL lines to MASTER_FARMER_ERRORS: start (HP/MP, eat/drink), moving away from a threat, each item used, the client refusing an item, no usable food / water in the bags, and done. The halt and each item use are probe points, and with the Crash Recorder box ticked the recorder is armed at the start of every rest, so a crash inside the rest names the call it died in. |
| 2.68.0 | **Crash recorder covers grinding combat.** Two game crashes on 2026-09-27 (11:49:55, v2.65.0, closing on a Young Wolf at 44 yd; 12:02:24, v2.67.0, casting Frostbolt with a wolf running in at 23 yd, mp 39) left no call name: the grind path, the rotation dispatcher, the smart rotation and the per-frame movement pulse had no probe points, so even an armed recorder could not name the native call. Probes added: `g:` ensure_target / auto_attack / combat_engage / face / scan / rotation.tick, `r:` acquire / dismount / auto_attack / face / smart, `sm:` range and cast per spell, racials, pet attack, `mv:` sentinel watch / walker / arbitrate. With the Crash Recorder ticked the recorder now stays armed for the whole of every grinding fight; the arm INFO line is written once per window, not on every extension. Probes cost nothing when the box is unticked. |
| 2.69.0 | **Crash Recorder moved to the General tab.** It sat on the Questing tab, so it was never ticked for the grinding crashes: the 12:07:48 crash (v2.68.0, mage casting Fireball at 29 yd, mp 27) again had no PROBE lines. The new checkpoints still showed the last completed tick: `r:smart > sm:range Fireball > sm:cast Fireball > sm:cast done`, then the character casting - the crash came in the frames of the cast, not in a bot decision. |
